Three-day stop: claude-plan 429 (2026-10-16 13:55Z → 2026-10-19 18:30Z).

The watchdog's single relaunch (sec-891, 14:01:51Z) failed the same way, and the coordinator stopped too, so nothing ran for about three days. The old logs are kept as $L/<name>.log.prev-20261016-429.
  • On 10-19 at 18:30Z the coordinator resumed. The plan provider answered a smoke test (PLAN-OK), the VM “macOS 3” was stopped with its lock gone, and main hadn't moved since #930 (postmerge green).
  • Meanwhile citadelculture (Travis's other initiative) had opened many PRs on our backlog, and outside contributors had opened #946 and #952–#966. The coordinator didn't duplicate them: acct-912 was not relaunched because #957 covers #912. They were landed or closed under D36/D45 (section 8.0a).

  • Smaller provider failures since: sec-937 died three times on 10-19/20 (stream disconnect, then an empty stream after 5 reconnects, then an empty stream after reading a big review). Each time it was relaunched with a “lean context, small steps, long outputs to files” note.
If 429 recurs: don't relaunch in a loop. Wait out the cooldown, smoke-test the provider with one short corbanu exec, then relaunch from RECOVERY NOTEs up to the cap. Workers' PRs and worktrees survive; check each one's last state before relaunching.
Drive-access outages (#616) — what is known (read before relaunching anything).
  • 10-12 ~11:01Z: first episode. Seven exec workers hung; fixed by #638 (a crashing turn ends with an error instead of hanging), installed since 10-13.
  • 10-15 ~07:59Z: the terminal lost access to /Volumes/CorbanuDrive (the claude-plan auth helper and the session store got “Operation not permitted”). Thanks to #638 every worker exited cleanly (qual-mod1, qual-aggr6, ci-win-tests, handoff-doc4), and the tmux server was gone. Nothing ran until the coordinator found it at ~20:49Z and relaunched them with RECOVERY NOTEs (old logs: $W/<name>.log.died-20261015T2049).
  • 10-15 20:59–21:01Z: the second episode killed main-ci4, qual-mod1, qual-aggr6 and handoff-doc4. The coordinator's own shell was unaffected. Travis's /vault add at ~21:06Z hit the same failure, and label kimi_api stored the error text as the secret (#855; vault-855 is fixing it). Terminal was relaunched ~21:05Z.
  • 21:13Z: fseventsd was at 32 GB RSS / 105% CPU after 25 days of uptime; CorbanuDrive's .fseventsd held 4.3 GB in 92,897 files. Travis OK'd a restart (21:22Z) and ran killall fseventsd himself at 21:28Z: RSS went to 14 MB and CPU to 1%.
  • 21:25:42Z, a separate event: T9, FASTDRIVE, FASTDRIVE2 and BackupBook were unmounted by a Finder/DiskArbitration unmount, not by this program. It blocked qual-aggr6 because “macOS 3” needs FASTDRIVE2 (section 6). Travis replugged the drives, and the coordinator mounted them at his request (22:46Z and 22:50Z).
  • Since 21:01Z: no episode. resil-616's read-only probes (every 0.5 s with system logs streaming, 21:52–22:10Z, then a lighter probe until 02:24Z) saw none at 22:00, 23:00, 00:00, 01:00 or 02:00. No worker has died of it since, and the watchdog has relaunched nothing.
Evidence so far:
  • TCC allowed Terminal throughout, there were no Seatbelt denials and no ES clients.
  • All episodes fall at ~:59–:01 past an hour.
  • resil-616 found that exec itself lost access first (reading its own session file), 0.1–2 s before the claude-plan helper failed. The helper is a victim, not the cause; a forced token refresh in a throwaway home changed no permissions.
  • Checked without a match: the Mac hasn't slept since 10-08; the disk manager logged nothing; Backblaze's scan runs every 10 minutes; a launchd job at :55 runs ~/.hourlywake.sh. fs_usage needs root, and there is no sudo.
  • The trigger is still unknown. #616 has been closed since 10-13 (by #638's fix for the hang); resil-616 only commented on it. Reopen it (or file a new issue) if an episode recurs.
Mitigations in place:
  • Workers on the internal disk with a watchdog (section 3).
  • #861 (installed since 395ac0adcd; in every build since): exec retries for up to --home-wait-secs (default 3600) instead of exiting; the helper exits 75. Every failure is logged to ~/Library/Logs/Corbanu/home-access.log on the internal disk: time, pids, program, operation, path, error, cwd, responsible app, sandbox check, mount, and the permissions/flags/xattrs/ACL of each parent. It never records contents or secrets.
  • A worker started while the home is unreadable still exits 75 at once (decision 11).
  • The rollback test to 45bfa7d938 is held (Travis 22:42Z): revisit it only if outages recur after the fseventsd restart.
At the next episode, read home-access.log first. On 2026-10-22 at 12:40Z the file still did not exist: no episode since 10-15 21:01Z. The 04:41Z/04:52Z entries noted in #894 were rel-858's test runs; it deleted them, and #895 keeps core unit tests from writing to the real log. #895 also lets the private helpers (apply-patch, the arg0 exec helper, the file helper) skip the home check, so they no longer stop inside Moderate's sandbox (#858); the credential broker keeps it.

1. How to resume (read first)

  1. Read this file top to bottom, then the binding worker rules: $W/sec-common.md and $W/wave2-common.md (archived copy of the 2026-10-12 versions in docs/management/coordinator-archive-20261012/), and $W/todo.md (the running decision/event log, newest at the bottom; wave 2 starts at the line ## RESUME 2026-10-13). W=/Volumes/CorbanuDrive/Corbanu/.codex-work/workers-20261002.
  2. Check live state, never trust this file blindly: 
    cd /Volumes/CorbanuDrive/Corbanu/CorbanuTerminal
gh pr list --state open --limit 50
gh issue list --state open --limit 100
gh run list --branch main --workflow postmerge-ci --limit 10
W=/Volumes/CorbanuDrive/Corbanu/.codex-work/workers-20261002
L=$HOME/.corbanu-workers                          # worker tmux server, logs and results (internal disk, section 3)
/opt/homebrew/bin/tmux -S $L/tmux.sock ls
$L/mon.sh sec-1261 sec-1064 sec-1309c fu-1323   # one line per running worker
ls $W/*.request*.md                              # requests waiting for a grant (section 3; numbered files too)
ls $W/*.hold.md                                  # orchestrator.rs merge holds (section 3)
ls -lt $W/*.signin.md | head                     # sign-in codes to relay to Travis at once (section 3)
cat $W/vm-macos3.lock                            # who holds UTM VM "macOS 3", if anyone (section 3)
cat $W/vm-tart.lock                              # who holds the Tart slot (free at this update)
tart list                                        # Tart VMs (TART_HOME=/Volumes/CorbanuDrive/Corbanu/.codex-work/tart-home); one at a time
tail -40 ~/Library/Logs/Corbanu/home-access.log  # #616 episodes (box at the top; absent = none)
  3. Read the result file of every worker that finished since this file was written ($HOME/.corbanu-workers/<name>.result.md; workers that finished before 10-15 21:10Z wrote $W/<name>.result.md), merge nothing a running worker still owns, and bring Travis the open decisions in section 11.
  4. Monitoring cadence Travis expects while workers run: check every 5 minutes (sleep ~285 s, or $HOME/.corbanu-workers/wait-event.sh 285 <names>), report briefly in plain language, and always list what needs Travis's decision with a recommendation.
  5. Times in todo.md's wave-2 section are UTC; the America/Phoenix clock on the Mac is UTC−7 (file timestamps in ls -l are Phoenix time). The coordinator's todo.md stamps drifted from about 1–2 minutes ahead of the Mac clock (date -u) on 10-20 to about 8–13 minutes ahead by 10-21 05:30Z, and its session log uses yet another clock, so order events by content when stamps disagree. On 10-22 the coordinator noted that its stamps from 11:47Z to 12:24Z ran about 15 minutes ahead; from its 12:36Z line on, stamps come from date -u.

2. Roles, tools and conventions

  • Coordinator (you): plans work, writes worker briefs, launches and supervises workers, reviews their reports, merges only when allowed, files follow-up issues, records Travis's decisions on the relevant GitHub issue/PR and in todo.md, and reports to Travis. The coordinator itself does very little code; small CI/test fixes are OK (e.g. PR #541 serialized racing tests).
  • Workers: independent corbanu exec sessions (Opus 5.5 High on the claude-plan provider) running in tmux sessions on the Mac. Each gets a brief file, writes a log and a final result file.
  • Reviewers: workers start their own independent read-only Opus 5.5 High review via installed corbanu exec -s read-only and iterate to APPROVE.
  • Functional/test runs inside products: GLM 5.3 Flash on Z.AI (-m glm-5.3-flash -c model_provider="zai"). Travis (2026-10-10): do not use GLM 5.2 without a recorded reason (it burns Z.AI credit). If the Anthropic content filter kills a security worker, relaunch it on GLM 5.3 Flash.
  • Language with Travis: plain, short, no jargon dumps; every report ends with the open decisions and a recommendation for each. Travis answers by number (“1. yes 2. …”).

3. Worker mechanics

Folder layout

Since 2026-10-15 ~21:10Z (Travis's resilience decision 8, after the #616 outages) workers run from the internal disk. L=$HOME/.corbanu-workers holds:

  • the worker tmux server $L/tmux.sock (tmux binary /opt/homebrew/bin/tmux);
  • per worker <n>: <n>.brief.md (a copy of the brief), <n>.log (the full transcript, last line EXIT:<code>; a relaunch keeps the old one as <n>.log.prev-<UTC>) and <n>.result.md (the final report);
  • the scripts launch.sh, mon.sh, wait-event.sh and watchdog.sh (below);
  • some evidence and review folders (e.g. mci4-evidence/, resil-616-probe/).

Still on the drive, in W=/Volumes/CorbanuDrive/Corbanu/.codex-work/workers-20261002 (outside any git repo):

  • the brief sources (<n>.md), todo.md (the coordinator's log), sec-common.md and wave2-common.md;
  • the request/grant files, the VM lock, go files and coord-merge.sh;
  • the briefs, logs and results of workers that finished before 10-15 21:10Z. An archived copy of the 10-12 .md files is in docs/management/coordinator-archive-20261012/.

Workers also put helper scripts and evidence in $W (e.g. vault855-*, acct-824-*, sec826-evidence/) and run folders in .codex-work/.

Launch command

W=/Volumes/CorbanuDrive/Corbanu/.codex-work/workers-20261002
L=$HOME/.corbanu-workers
$L/launch.sh <name> $W/<name>.md    # copies the brief to $L/<name>.brief.md, starts tmux session <name> on $L/tmux.sock

launch.sh runs this in /Volumes/CorbanuDrive/Corbanu: ~/.local/bin/corbanu exec -s danger-full-access -c approval_policy="never" --skip-git-repo-check -m claude-opus-5-5-plan -c model_provider="claude-plan" -c model_reasoning_effort="high" -C /Volumes/CorbanuDrive/Corbanu -o $L/<name>.result.md - < $L/<name>.brief.md > $L/<name>.log 2>&1; echo EXIT:$? >> $L/<name>.log. The old layout is no longer used: tmux server $W/tmux.sock, the tmux binary .codex-work/corbanu-terminal/bin/tmux, and everything in $W.

  • watchdog.sh runs in tmux session watchdog on $L/tmux.sock (since 10-15 21:11Z). Every 2 minutes it looks for a log that ended without EXIT:0 (or whose session is gone) and contains “macOS is blocking access” or “Operation not permitted (os error 1)”. For each one it waits until the Corbanu home (.codex-work/corbanu-terminal/home) lists again, then relaunches the worker via launch.sh from <n>.recover.md (a RECOVERY NOTE on top of the brief). It does this at most 3 times per worker (<n>.autorelaunch) and logs to $L/watchdog.log. It has relaunched nothing so far.
  • Code-blind acceptance workers run through a small script (e.g. macct-acc6.sh) with env -u CODEX_HOME -u CORBANU_HOME -u PFTERMINAL_HOME so they don't inherit the coordinator's home. Their briefs forbid reading source, PR diffs, worker logs or reviews.
  • Relaunch after a crash: the watchdog does it for access errors. By hand: prepend a RECOVERY NOTE to the brief (what's already done, branches/PRs, any VM lock held), save it as $W/<n>.relaunch.md and run $L/launch.sh <n> $W/<n>.relaunch.md, which moves the old log aside itself.
  • Monitoring loop (each round): line count and last line of every active log; EXIT:0 → read the result; gh pr checks for open PRs; investigate failures (job logs via gh api repos/CorbanuCore/CorbanuTerminal/actions/jobs/<id>/logs); report.
  • A good brief contains: what to read first (sec-common.md, related result files, issues/PRs), the problem with evidence, the owner decisions that bind it (quoted with date), the exact gate (regression test fail-before/pass-after, focused just test with and without developer-accounting, macOS clippy + Linux clippy -D warnings on glitch, one independent review to APPROVE, tmux functional check in a disposable home, short SOP video for user-visible changes), merge rules, “stop for sign-off if it changes Permissive”, cleanup, and “report as in sec-common.md”.

Wave 2 additions (2026-10-13/15)

  • $W/wave2-common.md — the short shared preamble every wave-2 brief starts with (read it before writing a brief). It points to sec-common.md and this handoff (sections 3–5, 9 bind workers) and adds: Mac builds -j8; on glitch check ssh glitch 'pgrep -c -x cargo' and wait while 2+ cargo builds run; one heavy build per worker; the #616 recovery rule (a reviewer corbanu exec idle at 0% CPU for 10+ min: kill only that PID, retry once); the reviewer command (installed corbanu exec -s read-only … -m claude-opus-5-5-plan, iterate to VERDICT: APPROVE on the exact pushed head); merge only when all checks are green with --match-head-commit; the security sign-off rule; cleanup; and the result-file format ending with “Decisions for Travis”.
  • wave2-common.md gained a CI update (2026-10-14) line: PR CI now runs Windows clippy as a required check (#730), so workers no longer dispatch rust-ci-full just for Windows clippy (only for Linux full-suite or remote-lane coverage); known flakes #673 and #615 allow one rerun of failed jobs (both since fixed by #742). Keep this file current when CI rules change.
  • wave2-common.md gained two binding rules on 2026-10-14 (both also in root AGENTS.md since #785):
    • REVIEW BUDGET RULE (Travis, 07:05Z/07:12Z): going over a review/evidence-check budget needs explicit authorization from Travis or from the coordinator (Travis delegated that discretion to the coordinator), recorded in the review ledger and an issue. Workers and integrators may never self-authorize (this supersedes the 09-12 “integrator discretion” reading that qual-aggr3 used). Mechanics: the worker stops the loop and writes $W/<name>.request.md (why, scope, stakes), then polls every 5 minutes for up to 2 hours for $W/<name>.grant.md — the coordinator's written answer. Check for request files in every monitoring round. If the budget is spent without a major defect, the worker records the last verdict honestly and stops that loop (as qual-aggr4 did).
    • KEYCHAIN (after the kc-758 slip, section 5): a disposable HOME does not stop third-party binaries (e.g. real Claude Code) from querying Travis's login keychain through macOS services. Such binaries may only run for tests/evidence inside a sandbox profile that denies com.apple.SecurityServer, com.apple.securityd.xpc and com.apple.securityd.systemkeychain; security must always name an explicit throwaway keychain path, never the default/login keychain.
  • Go files for milestone runs: a code-blind integrator prepares (candidate, packet, design, freeze) and then polls for $W/<name>.go before starting the VM; the coordinator writes it with Travis's answers to the pending items (e.g. qual-aggr3.go on 10-13). qual-mod1's first launch prepared and then gave up after 8 hours without a go file (21:51Z); the coordinator wrote qual-mod1.go (10-14 22:58Z, Travis's answers) and relaunched it from qual-mod1.relaunch.md (a RECOVERY NOTE on top of the original brief).
  • VM lock for “macOS 3” (since 10-14 22:59Z): only one milestone run may use the VM. The integrator that holds it writes $W/vm-macos3.lock (its name and the UTC time), and deletes it (rm, not rm -f) as soon as the VM is stopped and guest data erased — even if it fails. A waiting integrator polls every 5 minutes until the file is gone, then creates it. Holders so far:
    • qual-aggr5, which passed it to qual-mod1 (released 10-15 07:18:14Z);
    • qual-aggr6: 21:33Z, released 21:43Z when the VM wouldn't start, retaken 22:49Z, released 23:49:43Z;
    • pf60-s10: 10-16 03:10:03Z–08:06:54Z (the lock file names holder, time and VM);
    • qual-mod2 from 10-16 10:23:33Z (the lock was gone after the 429 stop; the relaunched worker finished 10-19 20:30Z), then qual-mod3 on 10-20. Nobody holds it now (section 3, “Wave 2 additions (2026-10-19/20)”).
    “macOS 3” (and “macOS 2”) have a UTM shared folder at /Volumes/FASTDRIVE2/z23. If FASTDRIVE2 isn't mounted, UTM refuses to start the VM (“A directory sharing device configuration is invalid”). Briefs tell integrators to stop and write a request in that case, not mount drives themselves. Write this rule into every milestone-run brief; a relaunched integrator first checks whether its previous life still holds the lock (the file names the holder) before waiting on it.
  • CODE REVIEW ROUNDS (coordinator, 2026-10-15, in wave2-common.md): the review budget is 5 rounds per PR (code, security or evidence) unless the brief names another number; a 6th round needs a request file, as for evidence checks; re-reviews of a merge commit only are cheap but still count. Added after mod-766 took 9 rounds with no budget named. Requests and grants so far (in $W; the worker writes <name>.request.md and polls; the coordinator answers in <name>.grant.md and records it in todo.md, the PR and the worker's review ledger):
    • perm-739 (10-15 01:11Z): r6 for #813's merge commit only.
    • qual-aggr6 (21:43Z): not a budget request but an environment blocker, FASTDRIVE2 unmounted. Resolved 22:46Z after Travis replugged it.
    • resil-616 (10-16 01:19Z): r6 for #861's test-only Windows cfg(unix) fix, diff 12031506c8..f340eea36c. At most one rerun for the unrelated Windows flakes, noted on #865.
    • pf60-s10 (02:30Z): request 1 asked for a fresh signed build. Answer (a): the coordinator rebuilt it, “INSTALLED 395ac0adcd at 03:08Z” is appended, and D3 is not granted (a later “D3 GRANTED” line would allow it).
    • mod-fix2 (04:20Z): r6 for #841, on condition that any further bypass means dropping the sed relaxation and shipping only the MOD-30 wording. An addendum (04:41Z) granted a merge-only re-review of PR #892's conflict merge; #892 merged 05:38Z.
    • sec-885 (10:47Z): r6 for #899 (the fail-closed fix for a sed e hidden in single quotes inside a double-quoted ${…}). r6 found the same trick inside arithmetic; the fix 28e9f28741 was pushed unreviewed. Grant 2 (12:15Z): r7 on 729a57bd4b..28e9f28741 only, merge if APPROVE and green, else fix fail-closed and stop. Worker sec-885b runs it.
  • Helper scripts (coordinator tools, not used by workers). Use the copies in $HOME/.corbanu-workers for current workers; they read $L/<name>.log, and $L/wait-event.sh reports a stall after 3600 s (its header comment says 900 s; the code uses 3600). The $W copies read $W logs (older workers). coord-merge.sh stays in $W.
    • mon.sh <names…> — one line per worker: log line count, seconds since the log last changed, count of panicked at model-provider lines (#616), and the last log line. (mon2.sh is an older variant.)
    • wait-event.sh <max-seconds> <names…> — blocks until a worker's log ends with EXIT:, a log stops changing for more than 3600 s, or a model-provider panic appears; then prints EVENT exit|stall|panic <name> and a mon.sh snapshot (or “no event” after the timeout). Used for the 5-minute check-in loop.
    • coord-merge.sh <PR> <review-output-file> — for the coordinator's own small main-repair PRs: waits for a VERDICT: line (must be APPROVE), for all PR checks to be green (re-runs failed checks once) and — only if a rust-ci-full run exists on the PR branch — for its Windows clippy job to pass, then merges with --match-head-commit. Since #730 PR checks include Windows clippy, so no dispatch is needed first.
  • Installed binary used for workers and reviewers: ~/.local/bin/corbanu (launcher script) runs main aa35e3f936 (installed 2026-10-21 04:09Z; INSTALL.json). Details in section 7.
  • Times in todo.md's wave-2 section are UTC.

Wave 2 additions (2026-10-19/20)

  • Six-worker cap (coordinator, 10-19 after the 429 stop): at most 6 concurrent Opus workers, with each worker's reviewers counted in its own budget. A worker that only polls CI still counts. At this update 4 worker sessions are open (section 10), so two more may start.
  • MEMORY RULE (coordinator, 10-19, in wave2-common.md; section 5): nothing on the Mac may be expected to exhaust memory. Bounded inputs plus counters, or glitch under MemoryMax=8G/prlimit. Kill a local test process above 16 GB RSS (that PID only). Put this rule in every brief that runs fail-before tests.
  • DECLINE RULE (Travis, 10-19/20, in wave2-common.md): whenever we decline or close a PR, ours or a contributor's, post a detailed written explanation on it. It says what the PR does, exactly why it is declined (evidence, failing checks, security reasoning), what version would be accepted, and links the issue/decision. Be courteous to outside contributors. Examples: #956, #966, #967 (land-ext1) and #880 (land-cc6).
  • Outside PRs: D36/D45 set the pattern. A land worker assesses each PR, and the coordinator brings Travis a merge-or-close list. Each merge gets an independent review and the normal gate, and fixes are pushed to the contributor's branch when they allow it. citadelculture's PRs (Travis's other initiative) are handled the same way, by land-cc5 and land-cc6. Never duplicate a citadelculture PR: check gh pr list --author citadelculture before briefing a fix, since several were opened during the stop.

Deleting the clone erases the guest, and throwaway keys are deleted (and checked absent) before the clone goes. TART_HOME=/Volumes/CorbanuDrive/Corbanu/.codex-work/tart-home (26 GB). Harness: scripts/qa/tart/, docs/qa/tartEvaluation.md. Don't touch the UTM VMs from a Tart run. The UTM lock $W/vm-macos3.lock still governs “macOS 3”. Its holders since #933 were qual-mod2 (from 10-16 10:23Z; the lock was gone at the restart, and the relaunched worker used the VM again until it finished at 10-19 20:30Z) and qual-mod3 (10-20, between its 03:14Z launch and its 06:26Z exit). Nobody holds it now.

A guest reaches it through an ssh -R tunnel. It is Travis's service, so never stop, restart or reconfigure it. It holds about 88 GB of RAM, which is why the MEMORY RULE matters. Use it only for protections a hosted model refuses to attempt.

Wave 2 additions (2026-10-20/21): coordinator standing rules

Added by the coordinator under Travis's delegated discretion (10-14) and applied in the grants listed below. Write them into briefs where they apply; the MEMORY RULE (section 5) and the DECLINE RULE (above) still stand unchanged.

  • Review-round discretion. When a PR spends its 5 rounds on real findings, the coordinator may grant 1–2 extra rounds, each with a stated blocking bar, usually: an end-to-end bypass of the specific item, anything looser than main, or a correctness bug (non-linear work counts). Further findings of the same class go to a tracking issue (#932 for classifier reader gaps, #943 for the .git check, #1159 for git output gaps) and don't block. After the granted rounds the worker reports and stops: if the last round finds another same-class item, it fixes it fail-closed, pushes, does not merge, and the coordinator (or Travis, as for D89 and D105) decides. Examples: sec-1084 (r6/r7), sec-1084b (r8), sec-1084c (r9 as a short check), sec-1141 (r6) and sec-1141b (r7). For changes that alter when a security hold is released, the coordinator may allow 6 rounds up front, and the design should start from explicit ownership (gpc-943b's #1104, 10-20 16:50Z).
  • Patch-id rule for main-only merges. If the only change since a PR's last APPROVE is a clean merge of origin/main, and the PR's own diff against main has the same patch-id (identical added and removed lines) as the approved diff, it merges on green without a new round. Compare git diff origin/main...HEAD | git patch-id before and after, and record it on the PR. Any conflict or changed patch-id means one merge-commit-only round. Applied first for #1070 (gpc-finish2's grant5) and #1061/#1072 (sec-937c, 11:36Z), formalised as a standing rule in ci-1030's grant (22:35Z), and used since for #1121, #1128, #1114 and the coordinator's own merge of main into #1135 (a test-file-only conflict where both tests were kept and the +/- lines matched the approved head).
  • rustfmt-only changes. A pure rustfmt or whitespace change that the coordinator has verified (identical tokens ignoring whitespace) needs no new review round (sec-zsh1b's grant, 10-21 03:50Z, for #1172).
  • orchestrator.rs hold (adopted 10-20 13:06Z, after #926 needed three conflict merges in core's orchestrator.rs, each in security logic). Before the final review of a PR that touches that file, the worker writes $W/<name>.hold.md. While it exists the coordinator merges no other PR that touches orchestrator.rs, and the worker deletes it after merging. First used by gpc-943b for #1103. Check ls $W/*.hold.md every round; none exists now.
  • Rust toolchain. The coordinator moved dev-build.sh, dev-build-online.sh and sec-common.md to 1.99.1 at 15:10Z (backups *.bak-195). Bump by hand every 2–3 stable releases or for a security release, following docs/rust-toolchain-bump.md (#1121). D101 (skip 1.100; measure 1.101 the same way) and D102 (keep the lint on nightly-2026-08-08) were answered on 10-21 (section 9).
  • Lockfile PRs are slow: any change to Cargo.lock (or MODULE.bazel.lock) starts the V8 canary Bazel builds, about 2.5 h, before a PR can go green (#1148 waited for them). Plan dependency bumps with that in mind.
  • Requests and grants since #1093 (all in $W, recorded in todo.md):
    • small-fix6 (13:42Z): one more rerun for #1091 (the #1102 timing flake).
    • sec-1084 (16:36Z): r6 for #1114, plus r7 only for another end-to-end bypass fixed fail-closed; keep the strictest rule; one extra round for #1109. Then r8 (sec-1084b, 18:13Z) and a short r9 (sec-1084c, 20:53Z) that found and fixed a quadratic eval rescan.
    • ci-1030 (22:35Z): #1121 (and #1128) merge after #1148 under the patch-id rule.
    • sec-1141 (02:57Z): r6 on #1158 scoped to ec43c98974; then r7 (sec-1141b, 03:24Z), which requested changes again (D105).
    • sec-zsh1b (03:50Z): option (b), the rustfmt-only wrap is covered by round 5.

Wave 2 additions (2026-10-21, since #1184): coordinator standing rules

Added since handoff-doc8 (05:45Z). The rules above still stand; write these into briefs where they apply.

  • CONTRIBUTOR-FIRST RULE (coordinator, 10-21 09:00Z, in wave2-common.md), added after contrib-1 found that our workers had duplicated 8 contributor PRs. Before writing code for any issue, a worker searches open PRs, drafts included (gh pr list --state open --search "<issue number> OR <key words>" and gh pr list --state open --json number,title,author,isDraft). If an outside contributor's PR already addresses it, the worker writes no duplicate: it reviews that PR and reports whether it can merge as is or what is missing, and the coordinator decides whether to finish it with credit. Workers record their searches in their result files. Contributor-review briefs also say: sign comments “— Corbanu Terminal maintainers”, never reveal internal paths or worker names, never push to a contributor's branch, post change requests as a courteous normal comment (not a GitHub “request changes” review), and don't mark a contributor's draft ready yourself.
  • Fork CI approval (D119): “Approve and run” is allowed for contributor fork PRs we plan to review, only if the PR changes nothing under .github/ (check the file list first); gh api -X POST repos/CorbanuCore/CorbanuTerminal/actions/runs/<id>/approve for runs in action_required.
  • Attribute-only or rustfmt-only test changes that the coordinator has verified need no new review round. Extends the 03:50Z rustfmt rule: sec-1141c's grant2 (12:04Z) merged #1158's head 7f8ac66010 (as 85bce28112) after the coordinator checked that it only added two #[cfg_attr(windows, ignore = …)] attributes identical to the file's existing ones (test file only), covered by round 9's APPROVE.
  • Sign-in handoff files ($W/<name>.signin.md): a worker that needs Travis to approve a device-code sign-in (for example with the D79 test account) writes the URL and code there. The coordinator relays it to Travis at once; codes expire within minutes (pf84-live1 approved its first one about 2 minutes after posting it). pf84-live1's two extra codes (for the optional mid-session removal check) expired unrelayed, so since 10:29Z the coordinator's round flags any *.signin.md changed in the last 20 minutes. Never copy such codes into PRs, issues or this file.
  • REVIEWER LAUNCH RULE (coordinator, 10-21 14:00Z, in wave2-common.md): run the independent reviewer (corbanu exec -s read-only …) in the foreground, or in a private tmux session the worker waits on, never as a backgrounded & job from the shell tool: several background reviewer runs died silently with no output (contrib-sec3 saw it twice). A run with no VERDICT line does not count as a round.
  • One build folder per worktree (sec-932): two worktrees of the same repo sharing a CARGO_TARGET_DIR once ran a test binary built from the other worktree.
  • Round-script fix: sec-1141c's request2 was missed for a while because the round script compared it with the older grant; coordround.sh now compares each request with the newest grant*.md. Two historical requests (mod-fix2, sec-d48) were closed with placeholder grant2.md files for the script.

Wave 2 additions (2026-10-21/22, since #1231): coordinator standing rules

Added since handoff-doc9 (13:45Z). The rules above still stand; write these into briefs where they apply.

  • MERGE GUARD RULE (coordinator, 10-21 19:10Z, in wave2-common.md): always pass the literal 40-character head SHA to gh pr merge N --merge --match-head-commit <sha>. Fetch it with gh pr view N --json headRefOid -q .headRefOid immediately before, and abort if it is empty. Never build the merge command in a loop where the SHA variable can be empty. Why: contrib-3's first six merges under D152 (#1203, #1221, #1224, #1225, #1226 and #1227) ran with an empty SHA variable, so the head check was silently off. The coordinator, or a contributor worker it briefs, may review and merge an outside contributor's PR when an independent review approves it, every check is green, and the PR changes nothing about security, Permissive behaviour, licensing or CI policy (Travis's terms). In practice contrib-3 and contrib-watch2 also required an Opus 5.5 High review of the exact head and no change under .github/ (as D119 does for fork CI), and read “security” as the classifier, sandbox, approvals and credentials. Limits:

    • Choosing between two contributors' PRs for the same fix is Travis's call (D154: #1005 was taken over #1232, both credited).
    • Thank each author, passing on the reviewer's optional suggestions; never push to a contributor's branch; sign comments “— Corbanu Terminal maintainers”.
    Merged under it so far: #1203, #1221, #1224, #1225, #1226, #1227, #991, #1210, #1258 and #1259; since #1281 also #1271, #1282 and #1272 (contrib-watch3).

Wave 2 additions (2026-10-26/28, since #1365): coordinator standing rules and lessons

All rules are in wave2-common.md; write them into briefs where they apply.

  • NO GIT STASH RULE (coordinator, 10-27): never use git stash (push, pop or apply). The stash is shared by every worktree of the repository: sec-d194 and sec-d191 both popped another worktree's “PF58 resumable source…” stash into their own worktrees (each reset only its own worktree; the stash is intact). For fail-before checks use git worktree add at main, git show main:path > /tmp/…, or a scratch commit that is then reverted. Never drop or clear the existing stash.
  • CARGO-DENY (10-27): fixed on main by #1407/#1408 (D198). The cargo-deny job checks out the PR head, not the merge ref, so a PR only goes green after origin/main is merged into it (a plain merge commit, never a force-push; for contributor branches ask kindly, or push the merge when maintainer edits are allowed). A main merge whose git diff <approved-head> <new-head> -- <the PR's own files> is empty needs no re-review. Don't edit deny.toml/audit.toml outside an owner decision.
  • CLASSIFIER MERGE QUEUE (coordinator, 10-27 23:20Z): post-taint classifier PRs merge in the order #1354, #1406 (D191), then the D195 and sec-fu PRs; each green run takes about 55 minutes and every merge forces a re-merge on the others. (#1415, D195, merged at 23:41Z, before #1406; sec-d191-2 reports it caused no conflict.)
  • BUILD DIR RULE (coordinator, 10-28): Mac build and target directories (CARGO_TARGET_DIR, Bazel output) go on /Volumes/CorbanuDrive, e.g. .codex-work/targets/<worker>, never under $HOME/.cache or elsewhere on the internal disk. Prefer glitch for heavy builds.

Lessons from this stretch (the facts are in todo.md):

  • Grant-file naming. Workers poll exactly $W/<name>.grant.md. On 10-27 the coordinator wrote the grants for sec-d191-2 and contrib-8 as <name>.grant1.md; at 19:01Z the slip was found and the files copied, and sec-d191-2 had lost about an hour. Related: contrib-8 exited at 22:23Z without carrying out the decisions in its contrib-8.grant2.md/grant4.md files (D199–D204), so contrib-9 was launched to do them. Write <name>.grant.md first (then keep a numbered copy if useful), and confirm the worker acknowledged it.
  • Workers don't re-read rules mid-run. A rule added to wave2-common.md reaches only workers launched afterwards. Example: sec-1428, launched before the BUILD DIR RULE, kept using ~/.cache/sec-1428-target. Tell running workers directly (a .grant.md note or the brief's message channel) and act on the machine where needed.
  • Internal-disk build dirs. The internal disk dropped from 59 to 47 GB free at 02:51Z on 10-28 (sec-1428's target in ~/.cache, 14 GB) and to 33 GB by 02:57Z. With no cargo running, the coordinator deleted that target (27 GB) and symlinked ~/.cache/sec-1428-target to .codex-work/targets/sec-1428 on CorbanuDrive. Watch free space (stop a worker's rustc if it falls below about 25 GB). ~/.cache/huggingface (124 GB) and ~/Library/Caches (47 GB) are not the program's.

Wave 2 additions (2026-10-28, since #1439): coordinator standing rules and lessons

NO GIT STASH, CARGO-DENY, CLASSIFIER MERGE QUEUE and BUILD DIR (above) still apply; one rule is new. All are in wave2-common.md.

  • WINDOWS SKIP RULE (coordinator, 10-28 10:42Z): never add a Windows #[cfg_attr(windows, ignore)] or skip to a new test without running it in the native Windows CI job first; skip only tests that actually fail there, with a specific reason and issue number. win-691c found 66 classifier tests that had copied the “Unix fixtures … #691” skip without ever running on Windows; 58 passed there (#1463), and the 8 that differ name #1461/#1462 (#1469 then re-enabled one).
  • CLASSIFIER MERGE QUEUE outcome: #1354 and #1406 merged first (10-27); then #1437, #1443, #1446, #1459, #1469 and #1481 in turn. #1446 had to re-merge main after #1443 landed in the same files and used a granted merge-only review round. The search-parser PRs went #1360 → #1363 → #1366 → #1364, after #1446, with main merged in between and each security review seeing the earlier ones applied; #1459 went in after #1363.

Lessons from this stretch (the facts are in todo.md):

  • Grant-file naming (again). sec-d217 renamed its first grant to sec-d217.grant-round4.md so it could detect a second one; the coordinator's round script counted that archived file as an answer, so sec-d217's second request (14:54Z) went unanswered until 16:08Z. The script is fixed. Keep exactly one live <name>.grant.md per open request; archive under a name the script ignores.
  • Workers don't re-read rules mid-run. Still true: the WINDOWS SKIP RULE came after #1455/#1459 had each copied another untried Windows skip (win-691c's report), and ci-maint restarted its nightly poll right after the coordinator killed it (12:09Z/13:09Z). Tell running workers directly and act on the machine where needed.
  • Internal-disk build dirs. Free space dipped to 32 GB again around 15:52Z and recovered to 49 GB on its own (transient). A user process outside the program (a node x-bookmark-backfill) held 12.7 GB of swap at 13:50Z. Keep all program build dirs on CorbanuDrive or glitch.

Wave 2 additions (2026-10-28, since #1487): lessons

No new standing rule in this stretch; everything in wave2-common.md still applies. Lessons (from the coordinator and todo.md):

  • Round-script freeze false positive. The coordinator's round script treats “Operation not permitted” in a worker's log as the #616 sign that macOS revoked access to CorbanuDrive. Sandbox tests print that text as their expected output, so a worker running them looks frozen when it isn't. Before acting, check the process itself (CPU, log growth, whether it can still read its folder); kill only a reviewer that really sits at 0% CPU with no output for 10+ minutes.
  • Dependency PRs wait for the V8 from-source job. A PR that changes dependencies (here #1498's notify bump and MODULE.bazel.lock) triggers v8-canary's from-source V8 build: on #1498 it ran from 18:20Z to 21:17Z. Plan for about three hours of CI on such PRs and do other work meanwhile; it is not a hang.
  • A main-only test lane can go red after a green PR. #1376's new ripgrep requirement passed PR CI because no PR job runs the Linux sandbox tests; only rust-ci-full (postmerge) and the nightly Bazel Linux job do. Main was red for about 3 h 20 min until #1500 (ci-rg). When a PR adds a tool requirement to tests, check which lanes actually run them.

Wave 2 additions (2026-10-28/29, since #1511): lessons

No new standing rule in wave2-common.md in this stretch; the MERGE GUARD RULE is now read strictly (first lesson). Lessons (from the coordinator, todo.md and worker reports):

  • The merge guard must abort, not just print. sec-1276b's pre-merge check for #1529 printed that main had moved by one commit (#1531) but did not stop the merge (05:05Z). #1531 only touches client.rs and its tests, so nothing interacted. #1530's merge used a check that exits before gh pr merge runs. Every guard (empty SHA, head moved, main moved when that matters) must stop the script, not log and continue.
  • Long wait loops hold a slot. noise-measure2 sat in an up-to-two-hour wait loop for #1525 before opening its test-only PR; at 05:01Z the coordinator counted it idle and launched flake-1539 in its place. A worker that has to wait hours on another PR should report and stop, to be relaunched later.
  • Coordinator clock drift. The coordinator's status times from 00:05Z to 03:35Z ran about 35 minutes ahead of UTC and were corrected later in todo.md. Take times from date -u or GitHub.
  • Measure before deciding on noise work. The W1 estimate (about 48 → 330 lines) measured 14 → 95 lines and 26 prompts once the real classifier ran over the corpus (#1513), and that corpus itself had a working-folder bug (99.4% of commands judged from /; noise-measure). Recommendations on prompt counts should cite harness numbers.
  • Rule slips, all minor and self-reported: sec-1516 used rm -f once on its own glitch log (reminder added to briefs); noise-measure2 ran pkill -f "gh pr view 1554" (matched only its own poller, still against the never-pkill-broadly rule); sec-722b started one short glitch rebuild while two other cargo jobs ran.
  • Model stream failures. win-1507 died twice on 10-28 (22:07Z: an Anthropic stream disconnect at 54k tokens with no output; 22:13Z: an empty model stream at once); a relaunch on a reworded brief (win-1507b.md) completed.

Wave 2 additions (2026-10-29 – 11-07, since #1557): pause, standing rules and lessons

New standing rules in wave2-common.md (coordinator, 10-29 10:07Z, after our #1558 and local-maxi's #1559 fixed the same issue): TASK NODE NOTE FOR ALL MERGED CONTRIBUTORS (post the factual Task Node reviewer note on every merged or finished-with-credit outside contribution, saying “no task id was named” when none was) and CLAIM COMMENT RULE (before starting on a public issue a contributor could pick up, post “A maintainer is working on this; PR to follow”; if a contributor PR appears anyway, prefer finishing theirs with credit). Lessons (from the coordinator, todo.md and worker reports):

  • A network drop kills every worker at once. At 10-29 11:06Z all six workers lost their model stream together and exited with no result file; nothing restarted them for eight days. On a resume, compare tmux ls and the logs' last lines with todo.md's list of launched workers, and relaunch each with a RECOVERY NOTE in its brief (what it already pushed or merged, what is on disk, that main may have moved), as perf-d261, flake-1448b and sec-1525c were on 11-06. Orphaned PRs of workers not relaunched were finished by fin-ours (#1572, #1576, #1578). noise-n12 was missed (section 10).
  • Sweep contributors first after any gap. 16 outside PRs went up to 8 days unanswered during the pause. The apology covered #1579–#1603 but not #1575, opened earlier on 10-29, which still has no maintainer comment; #1634 arrived after the sweeps. Use the SWEEP RULE's query over all open outside PRs, not a number range.
  • macOS nc -w hangs in an ssh Match exec. The glitch LAN probe in ~/.ssh/config used nc -w, which on macOS doesn't bound the connect, so ssh glitch could hang. Fixed at the resume by adding -G 2 (connect timeout).
  • Defender quarantines unified_exec/launch_path_tests.rs on the Windows PC (seen by perf-d261 and again by perf-label). Work in a copy of the file; never change Defender settings (briefs say so).
  • Duplicate work between workers and the coordinator. When RUSTSEC-2026-0441 (pin-utils unmaintained) turned cargo-deny red on 11-06, flake-1448b opened #1609 and the coordinator separately opened #1611 at 19:27Z; #1609 merged at 19:51Z and #1611 was closed as a duplicate at 20:08Z. Before a coordinator fix, check main and open PRs (ours too). The same applies to contributors: #1558/#1559 led to the CLAIM COMMENT RULE.
  • Check advice before documenting it. D291 asked to point multi-project Windows users to the elevated backend. win-dec measured that backend first and found it doesn't keep concurrent projects apart either (#1648), so #1646 was reworded and now waits for Travis.
  • A conservative rule ends a bypass loop. #1525 needed seven review rounds; rounds 5 and 6 each found another zsh bracket/arithmetic way past the check (quiet on main too). Round 7 approved a conservative rule: no arithmetic reading when the region holds quotes, \, backticks, newlines, #, [], {} or $(.
  • A red nightly on an unchanged main means flakes. Main didn't move for eight days, yet the nightly failed on seven nights; flake-1448b reproduced its three Linux causes on glitch by injecting them (sibling forks, a delay) and fixed them test-only, and win-1610 found #1610's cause with the Windows PC. Likewise cargo-deny can turn red with no code change when an advisory is published; open PRs then need a fresh run.
  • The Mac can't fetch the rusty_v8 fork's submodules (several GB from chromium.googlesource.com kept failing; sync-s0), so the sync skeleton runs on glitch and the Mac pushes the result.
  • Unique worker names. contrib-14 overwrote an older contrib-14.result.md (10-29); pick names that haven't been used.

Wave 2 additions (2026-11-07, since #1657): rules and lessons

New in wave2-common.md: the QUIET CONTRIBUTOR RULE CLARIFICATION (coordinator, 11-07): D151 means finishing a quiet contributor's PR with credit after about one week of silence, not about 90 minutes; trivial non-code asks (a release-note line, a regenerated snapshot, a main merge) may be done on their branch after a short note, with credit. D328 asks Travis to settle it. Lessons (from the coordinator, todo.md and worker reports):

  • Empty model streams. A worker can exit with EXIT:1 after its model stream returns nothing (“completed without any model output”) and leave no result file: win-1648 did so at 12:03Z (five empty attempts) and again at 14:00Z. Relaunch it with a RECOVERY NOTE (what is pushed, the open PR, what to continue from) and defensive framing of the task, as the MODEL-DECLINE pattern does; it continued from #1717's review.
  • The Windows PC may be off. Travis powered it off at about 07:56Z for a few hours. Workers then build and test only on native Windows CI (win-walk, win-fu3 and win-1708 did); Windows code can't be cross-compiled on the Mac (a crypto dependency needs the Windows SDK). Clean-ups on the desktop wait until it is back (win-gaps2's C:\CorbanuQA\win-gaps2).
  • The quiet-contributor rule was misquoted in briefs. Briefs on 11-06/07 said to finish a quiet contributor's PR after about 90 minutes, (the coordinator's brief for this update names #1580, #1598 and #1602 as affected); contrib-1107 found that D151 says about a week. The coordinator corrected wave2-common.md and raised D328. Quote owner decisions from section 9, not from memory.
  • Cancelled jobs hide real failures. From about 05:59Z every postmerge run on main failed only because the next push cancelled its waiting v8-canary job; behind that, the pf_30_s03 test had been failing on every commit since #1700. #1696 scoped the group per run; then the real failure showed and #1710 fixed it. Read failed runs job by job rather than assuming one cause.
  • A test can pass for the wrong reason. fix-pf30 found the pf_30 test's child inherited the parent's whole history (the spawn default), so it passed whether or not agent mail counted as untrusted. The new test starts the child without forked history and was shown to fail when the taint marking is removed.
  • Granted extra rounds can keep finding corners. #1698 had three granted rounds (6–8); each found new Low corners of the same class (zsh code-table writers). After the class-wide fix, the rest goes to Travis (D341) rather than another round.
  • Rule slips seen: sec-1685 ran pkill -f on its own script on glitch (it matched only that script); qa-1107 ran one early corbanu sandbox command on the PC without the throwaway home (one harmless log line); ci-cache6 ran git stash list once (read-only). deps-1106 stalled 37 minutes after a finished command and was relaunched with a RECOVERY NOTE after a targeted tmux kill.
  • glitch ssh: the Match exec LAN probe in ~/.ssh/config keeps nc -z -G 2 -w 2 (macOS nc -w alone doesn't bound the connect; fixed at the 11-06 resume). If ssh glitch hangs, check that line first.

Wave 2 additions (2026-11-07, since #1739): rules and lessons

New in wave2-common.md: the GLITCH TEST TIP (coordinator, from contrib-b): run codex-core tests with RUST_MIN_STACK=8388608, as CI does, and use a separate CARGO_TARGET_DIR per worktree (a shared one once ran a stale binary; dec-1702 hit a snapshot mix-up the same way). D328 (accepted 17:45Z) settles the quiet-contributor wait. Lessons (from the coordinator, todo.md and worker reports):

  • Sweep during busy waves too. Six outside PRs opened 15:00–17:20Z (#1736, #1737, #1738, #1744, #1750 by citadelculture; #1747 by 0xzoz) had no maintainer reply until the 17:48Z sweep; three contributor sweeps (contrib-a, contrib-b, contrib-c) then merged all of them and #1767 with thanks and Task Node notes.
  • Git's merge can drop our protections silently. S3a found main-only lines that the automatic merge lost without a conflict: network-proxy's check that broker settings can't change while the proxy runs, D355's proxied-Windows-elevated rule in unified_exec, and two linux-sandbox protections (refusing read-only paths that cross a writable symlink; releasing the start pipe). Each was caught by checking, line by line, for lines main added that went missing. Do that on every sync slice.
  • Measure before shipping a security plan. D368's plan assumed only full access lets a command borrow another's token; the measurement showed read/execute already does, and the change hung most elevated commands. Probe-only branches first, behaviour PRs after the decision.
  • Platform-specific test expectations. #1698's Windows failures were tests expecting the Linux classifier's results (quoting, path-named programs, the D191 temp rule); sec-1685d made each wrapped line match its plain line on every platform instead.
  • A claimed decision can still be duplicated. #1736 duplicated #1692 although D314 was marked claimed and done; offering to repurpose it (for D336) kept the contributor's work and credit.
  • Neutral framing after an empty stream. win-1560m exited at once with an empty model stream on its first brief; it was relaunched with neutral compatibility-testing framing and finished (as win-1648 did earlier with a RECOVERY NOTE).
  • Check whether a race is live. flake-tmux2 found the startup race in eight tmux tests was latent (slash commands like /status work during startup); the fix removed a hidden dependency rather than a live flake. Report which it is.

Wave 2 additions (2026-11-08, since #1811): rules and lessons

New in wave2-common.md: the INFRA RERUN RULE (11-08): during a GitHub/network outage a rerun of a job whose log shows only infra failures (HTTP 5xx, download timeouts, lost runner) doesn't count against the one-rerun allowance; up to 5 infra-only reruns per job, reading the log each time. The MERGE GUARD RULE now says in every brief: one merge per command, never in a loop (flake-1854 merged #1867–#1869 from one SHA-guarded loop).

  • Acknowledge contributors within the hour. #1804 (03:19Z) and #1841 (09:58Z) waited until 15:48Z for a first reply. Since 16:05Z the coordinator runs $HOME/.corbanu-workers/contrib-ack.sh each round: it lists open, non-draft fork PRs with no comment or review from anyone but the author. A watcher's poll treats a coordinator-acknowledged PR as answered, so queue its review separately (contrib-l for #1888 and #1892).
  • Blob size counts on main, not just on the PR. #1841's merge pushed a test file past 512,000 bytes; the policy only checks files a push changes, so main went red once and later pushes passed. Watch large test files (tainted_action_tests.rs) and move tests out before they reach the limit.
  • Tests that get slower each run. flake-1873 found the D311 slowdown was permanent %TEMP% grants left by legacy-session tests (25 per whole-crate run), not runner speed: check the shared parent folder's permission count first (#1890).
  • Windows compile errors. The Mac can't build the Windows code; a native Windows scratch run (about 6 minutes for one crate) is the quickest fail-before proof (win-1830, win-1861).

Wave 2 additions (2026-11-09, since #1900): rules and lessons

New in wave2-common.md:

  • SECURITY APPROVAL RULE (D318, 11-09). main requires a code-owner review for every path owned by @corbanu-security in .github/CODEOWNERS. gh pr view N --json reviewDecision,mergeStateStatus shows REVIEW_REQUIRED/BLOCKED when one is needed. The steps:
    1. The independent read-only Opus 5.5 High review reaches VERDICT: APPROVE on the exact pushed head.
    2. The worker (never the reviewer model) re-reads headRefOid and posts gh pr review N --approve as corbanu-security, citing the verdict, head SHA and round. The token is fetched only inside that command (vault label first, 0600 file as fallback) and never goes to a model, file, log or trace.
    3. Right before gh pr merge … --match-head-commit, the worker checks that the latest corbanu-security approval's commit_id equals that SHA and that the review's author really is corbanu-security.
    A new push needs a new review and approval. A pure main merge that leaves the PR's own files unchanged needs only a fresh approval citing the earlier verdict and the empty diff. Since 10:38Z GitHub also dismisses stale approvals (D463). --admin stays Travis's (D464). The coordinator ruled at 12:10Z that for test- or docs-only PRs the final independent Opus APPROVE of the exact head is enough for the approval, without a separate security review.
  • BLOB MERGE RULE (11-09). Before merging a PR that touches a file near the 512,000-byte limit (e.g. tainted_action_tests.rs), check its size on the merged result (current main plus the PR), not only on the PR head; if it would pass the limit, move tests to a child file first. This came from contrib-k's #1886 lesson.
  • RELEASE NOTE FRAGMENT RULE (D431). It takes effect once #1926 is on main (check ls qa/release/unreleased/notes there). The shared qa/release/unreleased/RELEASE_NOTES.md conflicted between parallel PRs and cost about 2 hours of CI twice. Under the rule:
    • Each user-visible change adds its own file qa/release/unreleased/notes/<pr-or-issue>-<slug>.md, starting with one ## Section heading copied exactly from an existing one.
    • To change an entry already on main, edit the fragment that holds it. Never recreate the shared file: repo-checks fails if it exists.
    • python3 scripts/release/release_notes.py check and preview validate and show the result.
    • At release time, release_notes.py assemble --version X writes qa/release/<version>/RELEASE_NOTES.md, the file the release workflow already reads.
    • #1926 migrates the 38 existing sections, and its preview reproduces the old file exactly.
    • A PR branched before #1926 that edited the old file gets a modify/delete conflict on its next main merge. Resolve it by git rm of the old file and moving only that PR's own entries into a fragment. Open PRs that edit the old file (gh pr diff, about 14:00Z): #1800, #1851, #1932, #1934, #1935–#1939, #1944 and #1949.
  • Vault label in worker shells. corbanu_security_pat resolves from the coordinator's shell and a plain worker tmux shell, but not inside a worker's corbanu exec agent shell (08:47Z; probably an environment or vault-access difference in agent shells). Until that is investigated, workers use the 0600 file fallback, and D466 (delete the file) waits.
  • Rule slips, all harmless:
    • sec-tar typed rm -f /dev/null on glitch; it ran as a non-root user, failed and changed nothing. It also used pkill -P on its own build script's children; another worker's cargo build kept running.
    • sync-main1's Windows target install let rustup self-update on glitch (1.29.1 → 1.29.3, outside ~/corbanu-rtx); self-update is now off.
    • dec-1109a shared one glitch build folder between two worktrees for about 20 minutes (a false compile error, rerun).
    • #1916 merged after #1914 without a corbanu-security approval; it is test-only and GitHub didn't block it.
    Next briefs repeat the rules: never rm -f (use ~/.Trash), never pkill.
  • Windows PC over SSH: the “connection is not using a post-quantum key exchange algorithm” warning is cosmetic (the PC's OpenSSH server); ignore it.
  • A contributor can arrive first. 0xzoz opened #1963 for #1946 three minutes after sec-1946 posted its claim. The coordinator stopped sec-1946 (nothing pushed) and launched rev-1963 to review and land the contributor's PR (CONTRIBUTOR-FIRST RULE).

Wave 2 additions (2026-11-09/10, since #1965): rules and lessons

New in wave2-common.md:

  • STANDING EXTRA-ROUND GRANT (coordinator, 11-10 00:32Z, under the owner's grant of discretion over additional evidence checks). When a PR's 5-round review budget is spent and the only changes since the last APPROVE are (a) a merge of the base branch, (b) moving release-note entries into qa/release/unreleased/notes/ fragments, and/or (c) a lint, format or argument-comment fix with no behaviour change, one extra short read-only Opus 5.5 High round scoped to exactly those changes is pre-authorized, with no request file. The reviewer must also confirm the PR's own behaviour is unchanged; then the corbanu-security approval is posted on the exact head if the PR is code-owned. Record “standing extra-round grant used” in the PR comment and the result file. New code, review findings or a second extra round still need a request file. Before it, the coordinator granted single sixth rounds by hand: #2003 (win-1976), #2004 (sec-1947) and #2006 (sec-1912b).
  • SECURITY APPROVAL RULE AMENDMENT (coordinator, 11-10). merge-train1 found that GitHub's API can report an old corbanu-security approval as being on a newer head (#1954, #1971), so the commit_id check alone can pass when nobody approved the new head. The approval body must now name the full head SHA (“Approves <40-char SHA> per independent Opus 5.5 High review round N”). Before merging, check both that the approval's commit_id equals the head and that its body names that exact SHA; if either fails, post a fresh approval on the exact head first. merge-train2 also noted that on sync-branch PRs the API reports the current head for every review (no stale dismissal there), so only the body check means anything on that branch.
  • RELEASE NOTE FRAGMENT RULE (D431) is in force: #1926 merged at 22:37Z on 11-09. Section 3's 11-09 block above has the procedure. Since then: #1800 and #1851 got the fragment heads-up; win-1976's #2003 moved its entry into a fragment at its main merge; doc23's second PR adds the missing fragments for #1920 and #2006.
  • Contributor CI approval for first-time contributors. GitHub holds a first-time contributor's workflow runs as action_required. The coordinator approves them only after reading the diff (what files, and whether the change could spawn processes, reach the network, read the environment or write files): envcoding's #1986 (two test files, pure classifier unit tests; approved 18:55Z, re-approved at 20:50Z and 03:05Z, the same two files each time) and ryssroad's #2005 (a lexer change plus a child test module, nothing that spawns, reaches the network, reads the environment or writes files; approved 22:18Z). Old action_required runs from llmontgo's pf-23-s01 branch were left alone (stale).
  • Advisory deadlocks. cargo-deny checks out the PR head, so when several advisories hit at once, each single fix PR stays red on the others. Land the fixes together in one plain merge of their approved heads (#1993 on main, #1995 on sync) instead of waiting for each to go green.
  • Out-of-order merges are fine when nothing depends on them; merge-train1 kept the stated dependencies (#1935 before #1936, #1932 before #1934, #1926 before the fragment moves, #1920 before #1966, #1977 before #1970) and didn't hold the rest for #1907's three-hour release builds.
  • Windows-only code isn't compiled by non-Windows CI. After #1935, #1939's Windows test hook no longer compiled; only the Opus review caught it. Two PRs that each passed (#1977 and #2003) together broke two Windows provenance tests in the non-blocking Windows integration job (#2016, fixed by #2017). Anyone changing Windows behaviour should check that job before merging (win-2000).
  • Rule slips, all harmless: merge-train1 merged #1937 and #1938 in one command (each still guarded on its exact SHA); sync-s1b2 typed rm -f once on glitch (on a file that didn't exist); win-prov removed its own PC folder with PowerShell Remove-Item -Recurse -Force, the Windows counterpart of rm -f; contrib-q overwrote its own log with echo >, which left NUL padding (never cat that log). merge-train1 also resolved sync ledger conflicts beyond “lockfile only”, each re-reviewed.

4. Merge and CI rules

  • Merge commits only: gh pr merge N --merge --match-head-commit <sha>, only when all checks are green and none pending. Never force-push. Never rm -f (use rm -r … < /dev/null). Never pkill broadly.
  • Don't merge a worker's PR while the worker is still running (it may still be fixing review items). Coordinator merges are OK when the worker has finished, or for docs-only PRs whose author is idle.
  • Code-owner approval (D318, 11-09): main requires a review from the code owner for paths owned by @corbanu-security in .github/CODEOWNERS (sandboxes, exec policy and approvals, filesystem permission policy and profiles, network proxy, security levels, credentials, dependency policy, the security CI workflows, the scripts only they run, the required-checks gate (blocking-ci.yml, check_ci_results.py) and the setup-ci/check-clean-worktree actions, release signing (D465), CODEOWNERS itself); 0 approvals are required elsewhere; admins are not enforced. Gap: rust-ci.yml and rust-ci-full.yml, which call rust-ci-core-security.yml, are not owned (changed too often), so an edit there can drop that job; reviewers of CI PRs check for it. After the independent read-only Opus 5.5 High review returns VERDICT: APPROVE on the exact head, the worker (never the reviewer model) posts gh pr review N --approve as corbanu-security with GH_TOKEN="$(corbanu vault auth-helper corbanu_security_pat 2>/dev/null || cat ~/.corbanu-secrets/corbanu-security-github.pat)" in front of the command, citing the verdict and head SHA, only after re-checking that headRefOid still equals that SHA (and confirming the review shows as posted by corbanu-security). Never approve an unreviewed head; every new push needs a new review and approval. Since 2026-11-09 10:38Z main dismisses stale approvals (dismiss_stale_reviews=true, D463); still, right before gh pr merge N --merge --match-head-commit <sha>, confirm that the latest APPROVED review by corbanu-security has commit_id equal to <sha> (gh api repos/CorbanuCore/CorbanuTerminal/pulls/N/reviews); if not, re-review and re-approve first. gh pr merge refuses a BLOCKED PR; --admin is Travis's only. Full text: SECURITY APPROVAL RULE in wave2-common.md.
  • Security sign-off: any change that alters Permissive behaviour (beyond owner-decided items) or the sandbox boundary in a new way must stop at an open PR titled “— needs security sign-off” for Travis.
  • Watch GitHub closing keywords in PR bodies (#433 once auto-closed #431). Use “Refs #N” when the PR must not close it.
  • main has no “branch must be up to date” rule. Merge origin/main into a PR only when it's conflicting, when it depends on newly merged code, or when a failure was fixed on main. Every push restarts ~40 CI jobs; needless re-merges were the main CI bottleneck on 2026-10-12.
  • CI structure: PR CI (rust-ci, core security tests on Linux+macOS added by #517/#556, Windows clippy since #730, argument-comment lint on changed crates since #771, tmux smoke, credential canaries on 3 OSes, build/package, blob size policy, repo checks); postmerge-ci on main (rust-ci-full incl. Windows clippy and full Linux test shards); nightly-ci (heavier jobs, incl. codex-core with developer-accounting since #567). CodeQL runs only on main/nightly — ignore stale CodeQL checks on PRs. A Cargo profile change triggers the v8-canary Bazel builds (~3 h).
  • Windows clippy runs on PRs since #730 (merged 2026-10-14 02:07Z, closes #717): cargo clippy --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc --tests --profile dev -- -D warnings, required (feeds CI required), restores main's caches but saves none (cache storage was over the 10 GB limit). Dispatch rust-ci-full on a branch only for Linux full-suite or remote-lane coverage. PR CI still does not run the native Windows Bazel tests (nightly only, #691).

5. Secrets and safety rules

  • Never print or commit secrets. Fetch at use time only: X="$(corbanu vault auth-helper <label>)" cmd. In disposable homes: env -u CORBANU_TEST_NO_NATIVE_KEYRING -u CORBANU_HOME -u CODEX_HOME -u PFTERMINAL_HOME ~/.local/bin/corbanu vault auth-helper <label>.
  • Vault labels in use: zai_key_3 (the only Z.AI key to use since 10-22 05:35Z; section 3), provider/zai_api_key (holds the key Travis revoked at 08:20Z until he updates it, D178b), provider/kimi_api_key, claude-plan-test-token, openai-api-key, glitch (glitch login password), drone windows password, provider/openrouter_api_key (used by acct-or for the live OpenRouter check), kimi_api3 (Moonshot key; kimi_api still holds error text until Travis deletes it, D18). Cloudflare publishing uses the Wrangler login already on the Mac (no vault label).
  • Never touch Travis's real macOS login keychain from freshly built binaries: every test/tmux/demo run sets CORBANU_TEST_NO_NATIVE_KEYRING=1 and a disposable CODEX_HOME/CORBANU_HOME/PFTERMINAL_HOME. The installed signed binary (~/.local/bin/corbanu) is fine.
  • Third-party binaries and the keychain (2026-10-14): in kc-758's evidence run a real Claude Code, started with a disposable HOME, ran its own read-only security find-generic-password lookup against Travis's login keychain twice (a temp-named item that doesn't exist; nothing found or changed, no prompt, search list unchanged). Since then (wave2-common.md, AGENTS.md via #785): run such binaries only inside a sandbox profile that denies the three keychain services, and give every security command an explicit throwaway keychain path.
  • Never use Travis's real ChatGPT login or real auth.json in tests. Since 10-21 a free ChatGPT test account exists (D79). Use it only in a throwaway home or disposable VM with the keychain kept out (CORBANU_TEST_NO_NATIVE_KEYRING=1 plus file-based login storage, inside a sandbox that blocks security), through a device code Travis approves, relayed to him at once via $W/<name>.signin.md (section 3); sign it out and delete the home afterwards, as pf84-live1 did (#1206). There is no test AWS account: Bedrock stays mock-only (D99 (b)).
  • MEMORY RULE (coordinator, 2026-10-19, in wave2-common.md): on 10-19 sec-937's fail-before test grew to 91.7 GB while mlx-serve held 88 GB, and macOS's jetsam killed system daemons (homed, secd, trustd, accountsd …); Travis got low-memory alerts. Never run a test or probe on the Mac that is expected to exhaust memory or grow without bound: prove it with a bounded input and a counter/size assertion, or run it on glitch under a hard cap (systemd-run --user --scope -p MemoryMax=8G … or prlimit --as=8589934592 …). Stop any local test process above 16 GB RSS at once (that PID only). Tart workers also stop if memory_pressure reports free below 20%.
  • Key-scan every diff before committing; dummy/canary secrets only in tests and evidence.

7. Installed build

  • ~/.local/bin/corbanu is a launcher script, the repo's scripts/dev/corbanu-launcher.sh (backup ~/.local/bin/corbanu.bak-20261010). It execs .codex-work/corbanu-terminal/bin/corbanu, a symlink to the real binary .codex-work/integration-build-20260922a/integration-package-dev/bin/corbanu. Install over the real binary, never over the launcher: at 10:53–10:56Z the coordinator briefly copied a binary over the launcher by mistake and restored it; sessions started in those three minutes ran without activate.sh's environment.
  • Rebuild procedure correction (2026-11-08 03:31Z, todo.md): when signing a new binary, codesign must pass --identifier com.corbanu.corbanu (short form -i), as the full command in the 10-22 entry below does. Without it, codesign uses the default identifier corbanu, and the binary can no longer read its keychain items: sign-in fails with “OS keyring load timed out”. A binary signed that way was installed 03:25–03:31Z on 11-08 and replaced. After every install, check that codesign -dv shows Identifier=com.corbanu.corbanu, and that a fresh-process corbanu exec signs in (qa-1108b did both).
  • Current install (2026-11-08 19:20Z): main 8cb852fd53, the merge of #1872 (#1827 nextest group), postmerge green (run 42509438095); installed 19:20Z per todo.md, signed with --identifier com.corbanu.corbanu (verified), smoke OK (SMOKE-OK), Rust 1.99.1, developer-accounting marker on, same Developer ID. sha256 begins 0f692d6df2a4bd2d. INSTALL.json: built_at 19:12:33Z, commit 8cb852fd53, previous_binary 01f15b0348, both sha256 and corbanu_sha256 equal the binary's; codesign -dv shows Identifier=com.corbanu.corbanu.   • Worker infrastructure on the internal disk with a watchdog (section 3).
  • resil-616 (#861, merged 395ac0adcd, installed 03:08Z):
    • The sign-in helper exits 75 when it can't read the home. Exec then retries (1 s doubling to 60 s, a message at least every 60 s) for up to --home-wait-secs (default 3600). If the helper's own sandbox denies the read, it exits 1.
    • Items that couldn't be saved stay in memory and are saved at exit within the same window.
    • The message no longer blames macOS privacy, and every failure goes to ~/Library/Logs/Corbanu/home-access.log; docs/exec.md describes it. The TUI is unchanged.
